# Latency — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Cut a research run's wall time (the audit measured 25.5 minutes on Tamil) without losing accuracy. Part 1 makes the nine changes the human approved as having no accuracy risk (audit steps 0–4). Part 2 runs the three approved experiments, X1, X2 and X3, in that order. Each experiment ends in a keep or revert decision against pre-registered criteria: accuracy first, time second.

**Architecture:**
- **Part 1 (Tasks 1–15, one PR on `perf/latency`).**
  - **Guard first.** Task 1 pins a digest of every provider request and of the event-type sequence of all 35 rows of the replay matrix. Every later task proves against those pins that every request is the same: byte for byte, or, in Task 12, identical up to its live acquisition snapshot. It also proves that the order of the events a run records (`run.state.events`) is the same. Where a task moves a pin on purpose (Tasks 8 and 12), it says exactly which rows move and why, and proves the move harmless with a separate test.
  - **Instrumentation (Tasks 2–4).** Additive metadata and records only.
  - **Tasks 5–11.** O1, O2, O3, O10, O11 and O12 reschedule work and leave every request byte-identical. O9 (Task 8) stops asking a research loop's forced last turn, which changes requests only for a model that ignores the "last iteration" instruction.
  - **The run-wide tool lock (Tasks 12–14).** It becomes a `ToolGate`: a per-URL flight lock plus a run-wide commit lock. One run shares one HTTP connection pool and one robots.txt answer per host. This amends design decision D9, as the human approved.
- **Part 2 (Tasks 16–25, a second PR for the tooling, then config commits).**
  - **Tooling (Tasks 16–19).** An inert stage capture, a paired-live-run runner with the pre-registered verdicts, a stage-replay runner, and a target thinking-mode toggle in the evaluation harness.
  - **Paid steps (Tasks 20–24).** A human checkpoint comes before the first paid call of each experiment. The baseline runs come first, then X1 (stage replay), X2 (researcher without thinking) and X3 (planner at `high`). Each experiment is kept as one config commit or reverted.
  - **Task 25** records the results.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.12 (the main checkout's `.venv`), asyncio, httpx, pydantic 2, LangGraph, ChromaDB with the ONNX MiniLM embedder, pytest with pytest-asyncio, the replay matrix (`src/deep_research/e2e_evaluation/`), the per-agent evaluation harness (`src/deep_research/evaluation/`), and DeepSeek (`deepseek-flash`) for the paid steps. Node v22 and Vitest only for the web check in Task 15. Windows 11 with Git Bash: every command below is for that machine.

**Status:** Plan written 2026-09-30 by the spec-plan-author agent; not yet reviewed. Nothing in it has been executed on the branch. Part 2's paid steps have never been run.

**Spec:** There is no separate spec: the audit is the spec, `docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-30-latency-audit.md` (in this branch at `0b5f3515`). Its §0 table, §2 rows O1–O4 and O8–O12, §3.1 (O5–O7 = X1–X3), §5 and §7 are what this plan builds. Where the audit and the human's approval differ, the approval wins. The places this happens are "Ambiguities resolved here" A1 and A2.

**Sources of truth, in order:**
1. The human's approval of 2026-09-30 (closed decisions H1–H8 below).
2. The audit.
3. The code at `0b5f3515` = `origin/main` `f4282818` plus the audit commit. Every `path:line` below was read there on 2026-09-30, except where a line says "at the end of Task N".
4. For compatibility only, the spec of branch `feat/notes-progress-report-stop` at `1f186af0`.

**Evidence: how this document was checked.**
- **Prototype.** Planning implemented every task of Part 1 and the tooling of Part 2 on an export of `0b5f3515`. It then rebuilt that work as one commit per task in this plan's order.
- **Generated blocks.** Every code block below that creates, replaces or appends text was generated from those commits. Each "replace" anchor occurs exactly once in its file at the moment its step applies it.
- **Executed on a fresh export.** A script then executed this document on another fresh export of `0b5f3515`, step by step. It applied each step's blocks in order, refused any anchor that did not occur exactly once, and ran each step's command blocks.
  - Every Expected line of Tasks 1–18, and of Task 19 Steps 2 and 4, is the value that run printed.
  - Task 19's last two command steps were stopped before they ran, at the human's instruction to deliver. Their Expected lines say `[not verified in planning]`; the prototype ran the same code green.
  - Review 1's re-check (2026-09-30) ran Task 22's code steps from this document on a post-Task-19 export. Every Expected line of Steps 2–6 is the value it printed, and the full suite after them printed `5090 passed, 1 deselected`. That count was taken before review 1 added two tests to Task 17. The re-check also ran the review's edited tooling (Tasks 1, 10, 17 and 18): its tests pass, and `python -m tests.replay_digests --pin` reported `0 of 35 rows moved` on an unchanged tree and 27 rows at batch size 2.
  - Tasks 20–25's paid commands were not run; their Expected lines are the outcomes the protocol defines.
- **Full suite.** The executed run's full suite at the end of Part 1 printed `5043 passed, 1 deselected` (Task 15 Step 1). Review 1's re-check, with Task 22's code applied, printed `5090 passed, 1 deselected`.
- **Keep edits.** Every conditional keep edit of Tasks 21–24 was applied to a copy of the finished export, one at a time and stacked (X1-A or X1-B, then X2, then X3), and the full suite passed each time.
  - At batch size 2 the replay matrix accepted all 35 rows, deterministically, with no network.
  - With the X1-B bound, with the researcher's thinking disabled, and with the planner at `high`, no replay row's requests or events moved.

## Global Constraints

- **Where.**
  - Part 1 runs on branch `perf/latency` in the worktree `C:\Users\Rahul Krishnamoorthy\OneDrive\Documents\Python Scripts\deep-research\.worktrees\perf-latency`.
  - Part 2 runs on a branch `perf/latency-experiments` created from `main` after Part 1 has merged (the first block of the Part 2 section).
  - Tasks run strictly in order. Commit after every task that changes files.
- **Approved, no accuracy risk (the human, 2026-09-30), and nothing else in Part 1:**
  - O8 instrumentation: tool duration and lock wait, the research tail split, per-call tokens; it changes nothing a model reads.
  - O1: `agents.verifier_concurrency` 16→64.
  - O2: batched memory writes with one `save_many` and one ONNX session in finalize.
  - O3: run a page's owed re-extraction batches together.
  - O9: drop the forced "finish now" 15th turn.
  - O10: split re-asks run together.
  - O11: skip downloading .doc/.docx/.xls/.xlsx.
  - O12: reputation lookups in parallel.
  - O4: narrow the run-wide tool lock with per-URL single-flight, a shared HTTP client and a per-host robots.txt cache.
  - The human explicitly approved amending design decision D9 (the run-wide tool lock).
- **Approved experiments, in order, each with its own checkpoint:**
  - **X1:** verifier batch size 5→2, or batches balanced by figure count. Build the stage-replay-on-frozen-state tool first.
  - **X2:** researcher thinking disabled. Add the harness toggle, then run paired live runs.
  - **X3:** planner at `high`, last.
  - Paid live runs are approved: roughly 6–10 runs of about 20–25 minutes each, on DeepSeek.
  - Every paid step still waits for its experiment's checkpoint. Every change whose experiment fails is reverted.
- **Not planned (the human's skip list):** per-topic pipelining, research on the draft plan, speculative planning during the one-time check, lower writer or reviewer effort, lower concurrency, hedged requests, shorter output caps.
- **UI-free.**
  - No file under `web/` changes.
  - No event name changes, and no event moves in the order a run records them (`run.state.events`, pinned by Task 1). The web reads these events (`web/lib/run-state.ts:207-240`).
  - This does not pin how the live stream interleaves different topics' `researcher.tool_call` events. That interleaving already varies with timing, and the web only counts those events.
  - Event metadata gains keys only, and the web ignores them (`researcher.tool_call` is counted only, `web/lib/run-state.ts:218-221`).
  - The CLI never renders event metadata (`src/deep_research/cli.py:459-464`).
- **Byte identity, and how it is proven.**
  - `agents/prompts.py` is not edited by any task.
  - Task 1's guard pins every request and the event order of every replay row. Every task but 8 and 12 leaves it passing unchanged. Task 8 moves three rows (O9), and Task 12 moves the `full` request digest of 22 rows and nothing else (O4). Each is proven harmless by its own test.
- **No secrets, no live calls in Part 1.**
  - Never read, print, create or commit `.env` or any `.env.*` file. The one test that loads the strict config gets the literal placeholder values shown in the Conventions.
  - No step of Part 1, and no step of Tasks 16–19, calls a model or a search provider.
  - Part 2's paid steps are marked **PAID**. Each runs only after its checkpoint's written approval, and only off-peak.
- **Off-peak only.** No paid command starts if any minute of the next 50 falls inside 01:00–04:00 or 06:00–10:00 UTC, on any day. The runners of Tasks 17 and 18 refuse by themselves. For the harness commands, run the one-line check in the Conventions first.
- **Merge order.** Part 1 merges on its own before anything of Part 2. Part 2's tooling (Tasks 16–19) is its own PR, merged before Checkpoint A. Each kept experiment is one config commit.
- **Compatibility.** Stay compatible with `feat/notes-progress-report-stop` (section "Compatibility with the notes/progress/report/stop branch").

### Conventions every task uses

- **Shell.**
  - Every block runs in Git Bash from the worktree root. No shell state survives between blocks.
  - Every block that runs Python first sets `PY="/c/Users/Rahul Krishnamoorthy/OneDrive/Documents/Python Scripts/deep-research/.venv/Scripts/python.exe"`.
  - `PYTHONPATH=src` is on every Python command. The shared `.venv` may hold an editable install of the main checkout, and this makes the worktree's own `src/` win.
- **pytest.**
  - The test line is `PYTHONPATH=src PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 "$PY" -m pytest <files> -q -p no:cacheprovider`.
  - Expected lines quote pytest's summary without its timing and warning counts. `3 failed, 117 passed` stands for a line like `3 failed, 117 passed, 1 warning in 12.03s`.
  - `tests/test_config.py::test_the_evidence_verifier_pipeline_config` loads the strict config, so it needs the four runtime secrets to exist. Every command that runs it sets them inline to placeholders: `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY=placeholder TAVILY_API_KEY=placeholder LANGSMITH_API_KEY=placeholder LANGSMITH_PROJECT=placeholder`.
- **Full suite.**
  - `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY=placeholder TAVILY_API_KEY=placeholder LANGSMITH_API_KEY=placeholder LANGSMITH_PROJECT=placeholder PYTHONPATH=src PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 "$PY" -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider` takes about two minutes.
  - `1 deselected` is the one `live` test that `pyproject.toml:45` deselects.
- **Replay matrix.** `PYTHONPATH=src "$PY" -m deep_research.e2e_evaluation suite --tier controlled --repetitions 3` takes about four minutes, never opens a socket, and writes under `output/` (gitignored).
- **Pins (the B-pin rule, shared with the notes branch's plans).**
  - `tests/test_evaluation/test_config.py::PINNED_TARGET_PROMPT_FINGERPRINTS` hashes each agent module's source with `agents/prompts.py` (`src/deep_research/evaluation/config.py:280-288`). Any edit to an agent module moves its pin, even a comment.
  - Task 1 records the five pinned values as baselines. Every re-pin step then runs `tests/pin_fingerprint.py <agent> "<comment>"...` (written in Task 1). The helper reads the agent's value as it stands in the file, never a literal copied into this plan, so the step applies whichever branch merged first.
  - A re-pin step's Expected line shows `<agent>: <old> -> <new>` as planning's run printed it, with nothing merged ahead of this plan. If the notes branch merged first, both values differ: then only the pytest line after the step must match.
  - Replay pins (Task 1's guard, `tests/test_graph/test_reader_notes_replay.py`, `tests/test_agents/test_planner_reader_answers.py`) stay literal. They pin what models are asked, and no notes-branch change may move them without its own proof.
  - After a merge, the guard is re-pinned with `python -m tests.replay_digests --pin` (Task 1). The Compatibility section says which printouts are proof enough.
- **Edits.**
  - Every edit is "replace this exact text with that text". "Create" writes a new file with exactly the block's text. "Append to" adds the block's text at the end of the file.
  - An anchor must occur exactly once when you reach it. If it does not, stop and report it. Never improvise a nearby match.
  - Blocks use four-backtick fences. Their text ends with a newline unless the file's own last line has none.
- **TDD.** Tests are written first and shown failing, except where a step says the test pins today's behaviour and passes at once (Task 1).
- **Commits.**
  - `git add <paths> && git commit -m "<type>(<scope>): <what>"`. Never `git add -A`: only the files a task names belong in its commit (`output/` and `memory/` are gitignored).
  - End every message with the attribution trailer your session requires. Push only if your session's rules say to.
- **OFF-PEAK CHECK** (prints `OK` or `REFUSE`; required before every paid harness command):

  ```bash
  PY="/c/Users/Rahul Krishnamoorthy/OneDrive/Documents/Python Scripts/deep-research/.venv/Scripts/python.exe"
  "$PY" -c "import datetime as d;n=d.datetime.now(d.timezone.utc);bad=[m for m in range(51) if 1<=(t:=n+d.timedelta(minutes=m)).hour<4 or 6<=t.hour<10];print('REFUSE' if bad else 'OK', n.strftime('%a %H:%MZ'))"
  ```

## Review Focus

1. **Task 12, the `ToolGate` (O4, D9 amended).**
   - Pages: a read's whole section (decision, fetch, commit) runs under its URL's flight lock, so one page is downloaded once. Two different pages, and searches, must overlap.
   - Commits: every admission decision and every `after_action` runs under the gate itself (an `asyncio.Lock`), never two at a time. A plain lock still gets D9's original rule.
   - Locks: the flight lock is always taken before the commit lock, so no loop can hold the commit lock while it waits for a page.
   - Ordered state: the per-topic policy state the budget and the search/read alternation read stays untouched.
   - Tests: `test_the_gate_overlaps_two_pages_but_never_two_decisions_or_commits`, `test_two_loops_on_one_page_run_its_section_one_after_the_other`, `test_two_loops_download_two_different_pages_at_once`, the existing `test_a_page_two_loops_request_at_once_is_downloaded_once_and_admitted_from_cache`, and the unchanged `test_the_tool_lock_serialises_only_the_tool_section` (a plain lock keeps D9's rule).
2. **Task 8, the forced last turn (O9).**
   - What changes: the loop asks `max_iterations - 1` turns and ends `finished`, and the prompt text is untouched.
   - Three replay rows move only because the replay's script calls a tool on its forced turn. `test_skipping_the_forced_final_turn_drops_only_that_turns_requests` shows that a model that obeys the instruction loses exactly the forced turns' requests, and nothing else, on all 35 rows. In the audit's two live runs the model obeyed in every topic (Open issue OI-2).
3. **Byte identity everywhere else.** Task 1's guard must pass unchanged at the end of every task but 8 and 12. Task 12 may move only `full` request digests: its Step 5 changes no other pinned value, and the guard passes with them.
4. **Concurrency helpers (Tasks 7, 9, 11).**
   - `gather_or_cancel` must cancel the siblings of a failed awaitable and re-raise.
   - Results must be applied in the original order: batch order (O3), half order for errors (O10), group order (O12).
5. **Task 6, finalize (O2).**
   - One `save_many` for all cited findings, with the old per-entry loop as the fallback when the batch fails.
   - One ONNX model per embedding provider (the runtime builds one per run), built on first use and called under a lock, so worker threads never share a half-built model.
6. **Tasks 13–14, the shared pool and the robots cache.**
   - Each read still gets its own client: own headers, own cookies, own redirects.
   - With a proxy configured, there is no pool at all.
   - A robots.txt client error settles the host as "no rules", and a timeout or 5xx settles nothing, exactly as every read used to decide (Open issue OI-10 names the one cookie difference).
7. **Part 2's protocol.**
   - The capture writes nothing unless bound (Task 16).
   - The criteria are constants written before any paid run (Task 17).
   - Every paid step sits behind a checkpoint.
   - A failed experiment leaves no config change and no code it needed.

### D9 as amended (H2)

D9 read: "tool calls stay serialised run-wide under one lock (one body, one download)" (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-24-evidence-verifier-pipeline-design.md:47`, with its §7.2 bullet at `:381-383`). Task 12 appends this amendment to that row, to the §7.2 bullet, and as a note to the plan that built it (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-24-evidence-verifier-pipeline.md:28`):

> **Amended 2026-09-30 by the human (latency audit O4; this plan's Task 12):** tool calls no longer run one at a time run-wide. A read's whole section runs under its URL's own lock, so one body is still downloaded once. Each call's admission decision and its commit run under one run-wide commit lock. Fetches of different pages, and searches, overlap.

### Plan decisions (where the audit or the approval leaves a choice)

| # | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | O4 is exactly the approved three parts: the `ToolGate` (per-URL flight lock plus one commit lock), one connection pool per run, and robots.txt once per host per run. A turn's own calls still run one after another. | The approval lists exactly these (H1). The audit's fourth part, "several tool calls of one turn at once", is not in it, and the per-topic policy state makes a turn's calls depend on each other (Open issue OI-1). |
| P2 | O9 skips the forced turn with a loop flag (`skip_final_answer_turn`); the loop ends `finished` after its last tool turn; `agents/prompts.py` is untouched. | No prompt changes, so every other turn's request is byte-identical (Task 1's guard). |
| P3 | The byte-identity proof is a digest guard over the whole replay matrix, built before any change (Task 1). It holds three request digests and one event-order digest. | The audit's tier 1 (§7) names request digests. The event digest adds H6's "no change to event order" as a test. |
| P4 | O2 falls back to today's per-entry publishing when the batch write fails. | A failed batch must not lose the cited findings' memory entries that the per-entry path would have stored. |
| P5 | O4's pool is used only when no proxy is configured (`urllib.request.getproxies()`). | httpx reads proxy settings only for a client that builds its own transport. |
| P6 | robots.txt: a 4xx answer settles a host as "no rules" for the run; a 5xx, a timeout or an unreadable body settles nothing, so the next read asks again. | That is exactly how each read decided before (`src/deep_research/tools/web_scraper.py:244-262`); only repeated identical fetches go. |
| P7 | Three questions: Tamil (figure-heavy), Latte (local recommendations), Rome (prose). | The audit asks for at least three questions of different shapes, including one figure-heavy (§7 tier 4). Tamil and Latte are the audit's own runs. |
| P8 | Baseline: Tamil twice, Latte once, Rome once, all with the stage capture bound, so four runs. X2 and X3 add three runs each, for ten in total. | It fits the approved "roughly 6–10" (H4). Two more runs would follow the audit's "control twice on each question" exactly (Open issue OI-4). |
| P9 | Every paid run starts from a fresh, empty memory of its own. | Otherwise a later arm reads what an earlier arm stored, through source reputations, procedural guidance and long-term findings, and the arms are not comparable (Open issue OI-6). |
| P10 | The criteria are module constants in `experiments/live_runs.py`, written in Task 17, before any paid run. | Pre-registration: nothing can be tuned after the results are seen. |
| P11 | X1's tier 3 is the verifier's single live case. | The three controlled cases carry one finding each, so no batch size can change their requests (`src/deep_research/evaluation/cases/evidence_verifier.py:210`, `:313`, `:409`). The live case carries four (`:512`). |
| P12 | X1-B runs only if X1-A is rejected. Its bound is the audit's own example: at most 5 findings and 12 figures per batch, and a heavier finding asked alone. | X1 says "or" (H3); the audit's §3.1 O5 gives the example. |
| P13 | A kept experiment is one commit to `config.yaml`. A rejected one changes no config. X1-B's code, written only for its experiment, is reverted with it. | H4: "revert any change whose experiment fails". |
| P14 | Each treatment arm overrides only its own variable. The rest of the config is pinned to the baseline's: an earlier kept experiment is undone in the override. | Single-variable arms; the baseline runs are the only controls. |

## Ambiguities resolved here

1. **A1, O4's scope.**
   - The audit's O4 has four parts (§2 row 4); the human's approval names three (H1).
   - This plan builds the three. "Several tool calls of one turn at once" is left out and listed as Open issue OI-1. The approval does not name it; a turn's calls share the topic's policy state and budget gate (Ground truth), so call k+1's admission depends on call k's commit; and the original D9 decision rejected it (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-24-evidence-verifier-pipeline.md:7340`).
2. **A2, X1's "agrees at least as often" (audit §7 tier 2).**
   - The audit calls the control's repeated runs "the control's own noise floor". A literal mean-against-mean comparison would reject a treatment no different from the control about half the time, because the two means are estimates of the same number.
   - This plan therefore reads the floor as the *lowest* agreement between two control repetitions. The treatment passes when its mean agreement with the controls is at least that floor (Task 18's `summarize`), and, on average, it must drop no more figures than the most any control dropped.
   - This is the only reading of an audit sentence that changes a number. It is listed again as Open issue OI-13, so the human can tighten it at Checkpoint X1.
3. **A3, "time second".**
   - An experiment passes only when every accuracy check passes, and then only if it is also faster. For X1: the Context Check's median stage time across repetitions is lower than the control's. For X2 and X3: the targeted stage is faster than the control mean on at least two of the three questions, and the mean end-to-end ratio is below 1.
   - A change that is accurate but not faster is reverted: it would cost risk for nothing.
4. **A4, O8's per-call records.**
   - "Per-call tokens" is read as: every provider call of a run is recorded with its label, its start offset from the run's start, its seconds, its output tokens and its reasoning tokens. The record goes in `StageTelemetry.call_records`, which reaches the quality record's `telemetry` block.
   - The label names the operation: `plan_draft`, `plan_repair`, `plan_review_repair`, `plan_review`, `plan_confirming_review`, `page_extraction`, `owed_extraction`, `cross_topic_extraction`, `dissent_extraction`, `extraction`, `context_check`, `statement_check`. A call with no label keeps its operation name.
5. **A5, O9's stop reason.**
   - A loop that runs out of turns after skipping its forced turn ends `finished`, the reason it ended with when the model answered on the forced turn. Nothing downstream reads the final answer text (audit §3.2 O9), so the synthetic finish carries no text.
6. **A6, the O4 live observation.**
   - The audit's tier table asks for "one live run watching `lock_wait_s`" for O4 (§7). Part 1 has no paid step, so this observation is made by Task 20's baseline runs. They run on Part 1's merged code, and their `run.json` records the median, p95 and maximum `lock_wait_s`.
   - Task 20 Step 7 records the result. It is an observation, not a gate: O4 was approved as no-risk (H1).
7. **A7, where the experiment tooling lives.**
   - In a new package, `src/deep_research/experiments/`, which nothing in the graph imports, with its tests in `tests/test_experiments/`. It is run by hand, one command per protocol step.
   - The capture hooks live where the inputs are: the graph node that starts each agent, and `check_statements`. They write nothing unless an experiment binds a directory.

## Open issues (for the human)

1. **OI-1. O4's fourth part is not planned.** The audit's "several tool calls of one turn at once" is not in the approval (A1). It needs its own approval and its own design: the per-topic policy state would have to be split per call, and the ordered budget gate kept. The original D9 decision priced it at "high risk for ≤ 30 s".
2. **OI-2. O9's premise holds for the live model, not for the replay script.**
   - The audit rates O9 "None" for accuracy because nothing reads the forced turn's answer. A tool a model calls on that turn still runs, and its read is admitted: three replay rows read a page on their forced turn, which moves their pins in Task 8.
   - In the audit's two live runs, the model never called a tool on iteration 15 in any of the nine topics (Ground truth), so the change is accuracy-neutral on that evidence. A model that ignored the instruction would lose those reads.
   - Nothing after O9 can measure this again, because the turn is no longer asked.
   - Review 1 suggested scanning stored local runs. No stored artifact under `output/` carries `researcher.tool_call` events: the quality record, the report and the evidence ledger do not. The audit's two streams remain the evidence.
3. **OI-3. Paid calls beyond the approved full runs.** H4 approves full runs. Each checkpoint lists the other paid calls its experiment makes, and needs the human's yes before they run:
   - Checkpoint X1: X1-A's twelve stage replays, plus the verifier's live case.
   - Checkpoint X1-B: X1-B's twelve stage replays, plus the live case.
   - Checkpoint X2: the researcher's controlled suite, twice.
   - Checkpoint X3: the planner's controlled suite, twice.
4. **OI-4. Ten runs instead of the audit's twelve.**
   - The audit's tier 4 runs "the control twice on each question". With three questions and two tier-4 experiments, that is 6 + 3 + 3 = 12 full runs; this plan defaults to 10 (P8), within H4.
   - For Latte and Rome, with one control each, a control spread cannot be measured. Their margins come from Tamil's measured spread, floored by the constants in Task 17.
   - At Checkpoint A the human may approve two more runs (`latte-baseline-2`, `rome-baseline-2`). The verdict code then uses every question's own spread, with no change.
   - Review 1 recommends them: with them, no time or accuracy margin for Latte or Rome leans on Tamil's spread. Checkpoint A's message says so.
5. **OI-5. No combined run.** The experiments are judged one variable at a time. A run with every kept change together is not in the approved budget, and this plan does not make one. If wanted, it is one more Tamil run with no override after Task 24, compared to the baseline with `compare`.
6. **OI-6. Every paid run starts with an empty memory (P9).**
   - The measured times and outcomes are therefore those of a run with no prior memory, which can differ from a run in the main checkout. Production keeps one shared `memory/`.
   - This is what makes the arms comparable. It also means no paid run writes to the shared `memory/` directory.
7. **OI-7. Secrets for the paid steps.**
   - The paid commands load the strict config, which needs `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`, `TAVILY_API_KEY`, `LANGSMITH_API_KEY` and `LANGSMITH_PROJECT`. This plan never reads, prints or creates `.env`.
   - At Checkpoint A the human puts the secrets where the worktree can read them, for example by copying the main checkout's `.env` to the worktree root (`.env` is gitignored). Task 25 asks the human to delete that copy.
   - No agent may do this without the human's own instruction.
8. **OI-8. A historical plan is annotated.** Task 12 appends one D9 note to `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-24-evidence-verifier-pipeline.md:28`, so that a reader of that executed plan finds the amendment. If the human prefers executed plans to stay untouched, drop that one edit; the spec carries the amendment either way.
9. **OI-9. All five agent pins move.**
   - planner: Task 4.
   - researcher: Tasks 2, 3, 4, 7, 8, 12.
   - source_evaluator: Task 11.
   - evidence_verifier: Tasks 4, 9, 16, and 22 if it runs.
   - report_writer: Task 6.
   - The notes branch moves them too. Whichever branch merges second re-pins after the merge, as the Compatibility section says.
10. **OI-10. robots.txt cookies.** A host's robots.txt is fetched once per run (Task 14). A site that sets a cookie on its robots.txt answer used to hand that cookie to every page read of that host, each read fetching robots.txt again with its own client. Now only the first read's client sees it. Task 14's test shows this.
11. **OI-11. O11's one difference.** A `.doc`, `.docx`, `.xls` or `.xlsx` URL whose server would have answered with a readable format (by content type or a redirect) used to be read; now it is refused before any request, with the same `unsupported_document_format` error every other such URL ended in (Task 10).
12. **OI-12. O10's halves share their batch's slot.** The two halves of a failed batch run inside that batch's `verifier_concurrency` slot. So a failure can put one more call in flight than the semaphore counts, at most one per failed batch. With 64 slots and no failed batch on Tamil (audit §2 O10), the plan treats this as acceptable.
13. **OI-13. The plan's own thresholds.**
   - The audit fixes the shape of each test but not every number. This plan pre-registers these (Task 17 and Task 18):
     - X1: the agreement floor reading (A2).
     - X2 and X3: a review-score margin of at least 0.03; findings, sources and publishers within 15%; refused sentences up to the larger of 2 and 25% above the control maximum; a dropped-rate margin of 0.05.
     - Tier 3: a case margin of at least 0.05.
   - Each is the floor under a measured control spread, and none comes from data the plan has seen.
   - **Closed for cited sources and publishers:** the human ruled on 2026-09-30 to keep the 15% tolerance (H9).
   - The human may change any other number before Checkpoint A, whose message prints every one of them as the code holds it. After that, they are fixed.
14. **OI-14. Off-peak on weekends.** The 2026-09-24 plan's check refuses only Monday to Friday (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-24-evidence-verifier-pipeline.md:68-69`); the human's own statement of DeepSeek's peak hours has them every day. This plan refuses every day. It costs waiting time on weekends only.

### Task 1: The guard: every replay row's requests and event order, pinned

The offline proof every later task leans on (audit §7 tier 1, extended by H6).

**Why it exists.** The replay matrix runs the real graph and agents with a scripted model, search, pages and memory. Its manifest has 35 rows (`src/deep_research/e2e_evaluation/replay_matrix.py:3163`), and its completer records every request it is sent (`ReplayCompleter.packet_sequence`).

**What it pins.** For every row:
- `full`: one digest over every request, byte for byte.
- `timing_free`: the same, with each research request's live acquisition-state lines left out. Which state a request catches depends only on how the loops interleave.
- `outside_research`: one digest over every request not made by the researcher.
- The request count.
- One digest of the run's event types in the order its state records them, with their count.

**How it is used.**
- A task that only reschedules work must leave all five unchanged.
- Task 8 moves three rows and Task 12 moves only `full` in 22 rows. Each proves its move harmless.
- The step below pins today's behaviour: the test passes at once, and that is its point.

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/replay_digests.py`
- Create: `tests/test_e2e_evaluation/test_request_digests.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `deep_research.e2e_evaluation.replay.run_replay_scenario(scenario, *, root, session_id) -> ReplayRun`, `network_denied()`, `ReplayRun.state.events`, `ReplayRun.replay.completer.packet_sequence: list[tuple[str, str]]`; `replay_matrix.scenario_by_id`, `REPLAY_CASE_MANIFEST`.
- Produces (`tests/replay_digests.py`): `request_lines(sequence) -> list[str]`, `timing_free_lines(sequence) -> list[str]`, `request_digests(sequence) -> tuple[str, str, str, int]` (full, timing_free, outside_research, count), `event_types(run: ReplayRun) -> list[str]`, `event_digest(run: ReplayRun) -> tuple[str, int]`, `replay_run(case_id: str, root: Path) -> ReplayRun`, `replay_requests(case_id: str, root: Path) -> list[tuple[str, str]]`, `repin(root: Path) -> tuple[list[str], int]` and `python -m tests.replay_digests --pin` (rewrites both pin dictionaries from a fresh run of every row, and prints per row which of full, timing_free, outside_research, count and events moved). (`tests/test_e2e_evaluation/test_request_digests.py`): `PINNED_REQUEST_DIGESTS: dict[str, tuple[str, str, str, int]]`, `PINNED_EVENT_DIGESTS: dict[str, tuple[str, int]]`. Tasks 8 and 12 edit these two dictionaries. (`tests/pin_fingerprint.py`): `--show` prints the five pinned values; `<agent> <comment line>...` re-pins one agent from the value in the file and prints `<agent>: <old> -> <new>`. Every re-pin step of Tasks 2-22 runs it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the guard.**

Create `tests/replay_digests.py`:

````python
"""The request and event digests of one replay run (latency plan, Task 1).

A replay's scripted completer records every provider request as ``(agent:schema,
text)``. A digest is sha256[:16] over the sorted ``"agent:schema sha256[:16]"``
lines of a run's requests, joined by newlines. Sorting makes it
order-insensitive: a change that only moves a request earlier or later keeps
it, and a change to one byte of one request, or one request more or fewer,
moves it. Three request digests are kept per run:

* ``full`` -- every request, byte for byte;
* ``timing_free`` -- every request with its acquisition-state snapshot lines
  left out: the lines of a researcher request that print the run's live
  acquisition state (queue, attempted and read URLs, counters, candidate and
  read rows, evidence rows, recorded findings) as it stands at the moment the
  request is built. Which state a request catches depends on how the loops of
  a run happen to interleave, not on what any agent decided;
* ``outside_research`` -- every request not made by the researcher, byte for
  byte: what the planner, source evaluator, verifier, writer and reviewer
  were asked.

The event digest is sha256[:16] over the run's event types, newline-joined,
in the order its final state records them -- the names and the order a reader
of the run's events (the web app's run state) depends on.

The one wall-clock value a request carries is a seeded memory entry's own
``timestamp`` inside a ``query_memory`` observation (the ``memory-is-not-read``
row seeds its entries at run time); it is replaced by a placeholder first, so
a digest is about the run and not the instant it ran.

``python -m tests.replay_digests --pin`` (from the repository root, with
``PYTHONPATH=src``) re-pins after a merge: it runs every row, rewrites the
values of both pin dictionaries in ``tests/test_e2e_evaluation/
test_request_digests.py`` and prints, per row, which of ``full``,
``timing_free``, ``outside_research``, ``count`` and ``events`` moved. The
comment that says why is the re-pinner's to write.

Not collected by pytest: the filename does not match ``test_*.py``.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import re
from collections.abc import Sequence
from pathlib import Path

from deep_research.e2e_evaluation.replay import (
    ReplayRun,
    network_denied,
    run_replay_scenario,
)
from deep_research.e2e_evaluation.replay_matrix import scenario_by_id

_WALL_CLOCK = re.compile(r'"timestamp": "\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}T[0-9:.]+\+00:00"')
_ACQUISITION_SNAPSHOT = re.compile(
    r"^- (?:next_action=|pending_passage_ids=|candidate_urls=|attempted_urls="
    r"|denied_urls=|read_urls=|consecutive_searches=|next_required_support_type="
    r"|candidate_id=|read_id=|evidence_id=|recorded finding |passage read_id=)"
)


def _line(key: str, text: str) -> str:
    return f"{key} {hashlib.sha256(text.encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()[:16]}"


def _clock_free(text: str) -> str:
    return _WALL_CLOCK.sub('"timestamp": "<wall clock>"', text)


def request_lines(sequence: Sequence[tuple[str, str]]) -> list[str]:
    """One sorted ``"agent:schema sha256[:16]"`` line per request, byte for byte."""
    return sorted(_line(key, _clock_free(text)) for key, text in sequence)


def timing_free_lines(sequence: Sequence[tuple[str, str]]) -> list[str]:
    """``request_lines`` with each request's acquisition-state snapshot left out."""
    return sorted(
        _line(
            key,
            "\n".join(
                line
                for line in _clock_free(text).splitlines()
                if not _ACQUISITION_SNAPSHOT.match(line)
            ),
        )
        for key, text in sequence
    )


def _digest(lines: Sequence[str]) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha256("\n".join(lines).encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()[:16]


def request_digests(sequence: Sequence[tuple[str, str]]) -> tuple[str, str, str, int]:
    """``(full, timing_free, outside_research, request count)`` of one run."""
    outside = [(key, text) for key, text in sequence if not key.startswith("researcher:")]
    return (
        _digest(request_lines(sequence)),
        _digest(timing_free_lines(sequence)),
        _digest(request_lines(outside)),
        len(sequence),
    )


def event_types(run: ReplayRun) -> list[str]:
    """The run's event types, in the order its final state records them."""
    return [event.event_type for event in run.state.events]


def event_digest(run: ReplayRun) -> tuple[str, int]:
    """``(digest, event count)`` of the run's event types, in order."""
    types = event_types(run)
    return _digest(types), len(types)


def replay_run(case_id: str, root: Path) -> ReplayRun:
    """One repetition of ``case_id``, network denied.

    The session id is the suite's own first repetition's, so a request that
    names its session names the same one the suite's artifact records.
    """
    with network_denied() as attempts:
        run = run_replay_scenario(
            scenario_by_id(case_id), root=root, session_id=f"replay-{case_id}-r1"
        )
    assert attempts == [], attempts
    return run


def replay_requests(case_id: str, root: Path) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
    """Every request one repetition of ``case_id`` sent, network denied."""
    return list(replay_run(case_id, root).replay.completer.packet_sequence)


_PINS = Path(__file__).parent / "test_e2e_evaluation" / "test_request_digests.py"
_FIELDS = ("full", "timing_free", "outside_research", "count")


def _value(values: Sequence[object]) -> str:
    return "(" + ", ".join(f'"{v}"' if isinstance(v, str) else str(v) for v in values) + ")"


def repin(root: Path) -> tuple[list[str], int]:
    """Rewrite both pin dictionaries from a fresh run of every row.

    Returns one line per row that moved, naming what moved, and the number of
    rows. Only pinned values change; every comment stays as it was.
    """
    from tests.test_e2e_evaluation.test_request_digests import (
        PINNED_EVENT_DIGESTS,
        PINNED_REQUEST_DIGESTS,
    )

    text = _PINS.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    report: list[str] = []
    for case_id in sorted(PINNED_REQUEST_DIGESTS):
        run = replay_run(case_id, root / case_id)
        requests = request_digests(list(run.replay.completer.packet_sequence))
        events = event_digest(run)
        old_requests, old_events = PINNED_REQUEST_DIGESTS[case_id], PINNED_EVENT_DIGESTS[case_id]
        moved = [name for name, a, b in zip(_FIELDS, old_requests, requests) if a != b]
        if old_events != events:
            moved.append("events")
        if not moved:
            continue
        report.append(f"{case_id}: {', '.join(moved)}")
        for old, new in ((old_requests, requests), (old_events, events)):
            if old != new:
                line = f'    "{case_id}": {_value(old)},\n'
                assert text.count(line) == 1, case_id
                text = text.replace(line, f'    "{case_id}": {_value(new)},\n')
    _PINS.write_text(text, encoding="utf-8")
    return report, len(PINNED_REQUEST_DIGESTS)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    import sys
    import tempfile

    if sys.argv[1:] != ["--pin"]:
        raise SystemExit("usage: python -m tests.replay_digests --pin")
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as directory:
        moved, rows = repin(Path(directory))
    print("\n".join(moved) if moved else "no row moved")
    print(f"{len(moved)} of {rows} rows moved")
````

````python
"""Every provider request and every event of every replay row, pinned
(latency plan, Task 1).

The latency work moves when requests start, never what they say, and never
which events a run records or in what order. These pins are the offline proof
of that for the whole real-agent matrix. ``PINNED_REQUEST_DIGESTS`` holds each
row's (full, timing_free, outside_research, count) request digests and
``PINNED_EVENT_DIGESTS`` its (event types in order, count) digest
(``tests/replay_digests.py``), as they stood at the start of the latency plan.
A change that alters one byte of one request, or adds or drops one, moves
full; timing_free moves only when something other than a live
acquisition-state snapshot changed; outside_research moves only when a
request of the planner, source evaluator, verifier, writer or reviewer
changed. A task that moves a pin on purpose re-pins that row in its own commit
and says why in a comment above the entry or the dictionary.
"""
````

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the re-pin helper, and record the five pin baselines (the B-pin rule).** Every later re-pin goes through this helper: it reads the agent's value as it stands in the file, never a value copied into this plan, so a re-pin applies whichever of this branch and the notes branch merged first.

Create `tests/pin_fingerprint.py`:

````python
"""Re-pin one agent's prompt fingerprint (latency plan, Task 1; the B-pin rule).

    python tests/pin_fingerprint.py --show
    python tests/pin_fingerprint.py <agent> "<comment line>" ["<comment line>" ...]

``--show`` prints every agent's pinned value. A re-pin reads the agent's value
as it stands in ``tests/test_evaluation/test_config.py`` -- the baseline Task 1
recorded, or what an earlier re-pin left -- never a value copied into a plan,
so it applies whichever branch merged first. It computes the fingerprint of the
current source, writes the comment lines above the entry (the last one ending
"Moved `<old>` -> `<new>`."), and prints "<agent>: <old> -> <new>". It refuses
when the value would not move: a re-pin follows an edit to that agent's module.

Run from the repository root with ``PYTHONPATH=src``. Not collected by pytest:
the filename does not match ``test_*.py``.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import sys
from pathlib import Path

from deep_research.evaluation.config import agent_prompt_fingerprint

PINS = Path("tests/test_evaluation/test_config.py")
AGENTS = ("planner", "researcher", "source_evaluator", "evidence_verifier", "report_writer")


def _entry(text: str, agent: str) -> re.Match[str]:
    matches = list(re.finditer(rf'^    "{agent}": "([0-9a-f]{{12}})",$', text, re.M))
    if len(matches) != 1:
        raise SystemExit(f"{agent}: expected one pinned entry, found {len(matches)}")
    return matches[0]


def main(argv: list[str]) -> int:
    text = PINS.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    if argv == ["--show"]:
        for agent in AGENTS:
            print(agent, _entry(text, agent).group(1))
        return 0
    if len(argv) < 2 or argv[0] not in AGENTS:
        raise SystemExit("usage: pin_fingerprint.py --show | <agent> <comment line> [...]")
    agent, comment = argv[0], argv[1:]
    match = _entry(text, agent)
    old, new = match.group(1), agent_prompt_fingerprint(agent)
    if old == new:
        raise SystemExit(f"{agent}: the pin did not move ({old})")
    lines = [f"    # {line}" for line in comment]
    lines[-1] += f" Moved `{old}` -> `{new}`."
    entry = "\n".join(lines) + "\n" + f'    "{agent}": "{new}",'
    PINS.write_text(text[: match.start()] + entry + text[match.end():], encoding="utf-8")
    print(f"{agent}: {old} -> {new}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
````

### Task 2: O8, part 1: each tool call's lock wait and run time

Audit O8 (§2 row 8, §3.2): today `researcher.tool_call` events carry no timing, and all of a turn's events are stamped when the turn ends (`src/deep_research/agents/react.py:743-746`), so nothing measures how long a call waited for the run-wide lock or ran. The loop now measures both around each `use_tool` decision and reports them through a new callback. The researcher keeps them by proposal id and adds `lock_wait_s` and `duration_s` to that call's event, additive metadata the web ignores (`web/lib/run-state.ts:218-221`). No request changes; the researcher's pin moves because its module changes.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/deep_research/agents/__init__.py` (lines 236-239, 733-736)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/agents/react.py` (lines 14-17, 44-47, 350-353, 372-375, 417-420, 423-426, 603-611, 647-650)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/agents/researcher.py` (lines 2613-2637, 4438-4445, 4461-4464)
- Test: `tests/test_agents/test_react.py` (end)
- Test: `tests/test_agents/test_researcher.py` (end)
- Test: `tests/test_evaluation/test_config.py` (lines 1156-1160)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `run_react_loop(...)` (`src/deep_research/agents/react.py`), `tool_call_event(sub_topic, step)` (`src/deep_research/agents/researcher.py`).
- Produces: `ToolTimingCallback: TypeAlias = Callable[[str, float, float], None]` (proposal id, lock wait in seconds, run time in seconds), exported from `deep_research.agents`; `run_react_loop(..., on_tool_timing: ToolTimingCallback | None = None)`, called once per `use_tool` decision after its section, with values rounded to 3 decimals; `tool_call_event(sub_topic, step, *, timing: tuple[float, float] | None = None)`, which adds `lock_wait_s` and `duration_s` only when `timing` is given. Task 12 changes how the wait is measured, not this interface.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests.** A loop-level test with a held lock, and a researcher-level test that reads the event's two keys.

Append to `tests/test_agents/test_react.py`:

````python


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Tool timings (latency audit O8)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


class _SlowEchoTool(EchoTool):
    """Echo, after a pause long enough to measure."""

    async def _execute(
        self, context: ToolCallContext, **kwargs: Any
    ) -> ToolExecution:
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        return await super()._execute(context, **kwargs)


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_every_use_tool_decision_reports_its_lock_wait_and_run_time(
    tracker: Tracker,
) -> None:
    """O8: ``on_tool_timing`` hears, for each tool call, how long it waited for
    the tool lock and how long its tool ran. A call no tool ran reports no run
    time, and a finish is not a tool call. (Thresholds leave room for the
    Windows event loop, whose timers can fire up to one 15.6 ms tick early.)"""
    lock = asyncio.Lock()
    timings: list[tuple[str, float, float]] = []

    async def hold_the_lock() -> None:
        async with lock:
            await asyncio.sleep(0.05)

    holder = asyncio.create_task(hold_the_lock())
    await asyncio.sleep(0)

    async with agent_scope(tracker):
        run = await run_react_loop(
            agent_name="researcher",
            tracker=tracker,
            tools=AgentToolset([_SlowEchoTool(tracker)], allowed=["echo"]),
            decide=_decider(
                [
                    use_tool("Echo once.", "echo", json.dumps({"value": "a"})),
                    use_tool("Ask for a tool that is not offered.", "missing"),
                    finish("Done.", "The answer."),
                ]
            ),
            max_iterations=4,
            tool_budget=4,
            tool_lock=lock,
            on_tool_timing=lambda *timing: timings.append(timing),
        )
    await holder

    assert run.stop_reason == "finished"
    assert [proposal for proposal, _, _ in timings] == [
        step.proposal_id for step in run.steps[:2]
    ]
    (_, waited, ran), (_, unknown_waited, unknown_ran) = timings
    assert waited >= 0.02
    assert ran >= 0.02
    assert unknown_ran == 0.0
    assert unknown_waited < 0.02
````

Append to `tests/test_agents/test_researcher.py`:

````python


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_a_live_tool_call_event_carries_its_lock_wait_and_run_time(
    tracker: Tracker,
) -> None:
    """Latency audit O8: each live ``researcher.tool_call`` carries the seconds
    the call waited for the run's tool lock and the seconds its tool ran; the
    post-loop rebuild (``tool_call_events``) keeps its old shape."""
    completer = ScriptedCompleter(
        decisions=_search_and_scrape_decisions(), outputs=[_findings_draft()]
    )
    agent = _researcher(tracker, completer)
    state = _state(sub_topics=[_sub_topic("Alpha", 1)])
    received: list[ResearchEvent] = []

    async with tracker.session_span("session-1", "q"):
        with bind_live_sink(received.append):
            outcome = await agent.run(state)

    live = [event for event in received if event.event_type == "researcher.tool_call"]
    assert len(live) == 2
    for event in live:
        assert isinstance(event.metadata["lock_wait_s"], float)
        assert isinstance(event.metadata["duration_s"], float)
        assert event.metadata["lock_wait_s"] >= 0.0
        assert event.metadata["duration_s"] >= 0.0
    for event in tool_call_events(state.sub_topics[0], outcome.react):
        assert "lock_wait_s" not in event.metadata
        assert "duration_s" not in event.metadata
````

````python


def tool_call_event(sub_topic: SubTopic, step: ReActStep) -> ResearchEvent | None:
    """Report one tool call the sub-topic's loop made, or ``None`` for a step without one.

    Built when the step's observation is recorded (live-briefs spec E3), so the
    event is stamped at the call rather than when the sub-topic's loop ends.
    """
    observation = step.observation
    if observation is None:
        return None
    return agent_event(
        agent_name=RESEARCHER_NAME,
        event_type="researcher.tool_call",
        message=f"{observation.tool_name} call completed.",
        metadata={
            "sub_topic": summarize_text(sub_topic.title),
            "tool": observation.tool_name,
            "proposal_id": step.proposal_id,
            "iteration": step.iteration,
            "success": observation.success,
            "error_type": observation.error_type,
        },
    )

````

with:

````python


def tool_call_event(
    sub_topic: SubTopic,
    step: ReActStep,
    *,
    timing: tuple[float, float] | None = None,
) -> ResearchEvent | None:
    """Report one tool call the sub-topic's loop made, or ``None`` for a step without one.

    Built when the step's observation is recorded (live-briefs spec E3), so the
    event is stamped at the call rather than when the sub-topic's loop ends.

    ``timing`` is the call's ``(lock_wait_s, duration_s)`` from the loop
    (latency audit O8): the seconds it waited for the run's tool lock and the
    seconds its tool ran. Both keys are added only when it is given, so an
    event rebuilt after the loop (``tool_call_events``) keeps its old shape.
    """
    observation = step.observation
    if observation is None:
        return None
    metadata: dict[str, JsonValue] = {
        "sub_topic": summarize_text(sub_topic.title),
        "tool": observation.tool_name,
        "proposal_id": step.proposal_id,
        "iteration": step.iteration,
        "success": observation.success,
        "error_type": observation.error_type,
    }
    if timing is not None:
        metadata["lock_wait_s"], metadata["duration_s"] = timing
    return agent_event(
        agent_name=RESEARCHER_NAME,
        event_type="researcher.tool_call",
        message=f"{observation.tool_name} call completed.",
        metadata=metadata,
    )

````

---

### Task 3: O8, part 2: the research tail split

Audit O8: a topic's tail, from the end of its ReAct loop to its completion, is today one number. `extract_findings` now fills an `ExtractionTimings` record: the wait for the topic's page extractions, the owed re-extraction round's time and call count, and the slowest page. `researcher.sub_topic.completed` gains those four keys: `extraction_wait_s`, `owed_round_s`, `owed_calls`, `slowest_page_s`. This is additive metadata; the web reads only `findings_retained`, `successful_reads` and the coverage id from that event (`web/lib/run-state.ts:210-217`).

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/deep_research/agents/__init__.py` (lines 373-376, 883-886)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/agents/researcher.py` (lines 2680-2683, 2703-2733, 2928-2935, 3674-3677, 3714-3717, 3807-3810, 3814-3817, 3842-3845, 4165-4168, 4190-4193, 4540-4543, 4569-4572, 4655-4658)
- Test: `tests/test_agents/test_researcher.py` (end)
- Test: `tests/test_evaluation/test_config.py` (lines 1161-1165)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `extract_findings(...)`, `sub_topic_completed_event(...)` (`src/deep_research/agents/researcher.py`).
- Produces: `@dataclass(slots=True) class ExtractionTimings` with `extraction_wait_s: float = 0.0`, `owed_round_s: float = 0.0`, `owed_calls: int = 0`, `slowest_page_s: float = 0.0`, exported from `deep_research.agents`; `extract_findings(..., timings: ExtractionTimings | None = None)`; `sub_topic_completed_event(..., timings: ExtractionTimings | None = None)`. Task 7 times the owed round it changes through the same record.

Append to `tests/test_agents/test_researcher.py`:

````python


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_the_completed_event_splits_the_tail_after_the_loop(
    tracker: Tracker,
) -> None:
    """Latency audit O8: ``researcher.sub_topic.completed`` says where the time
    after the loop went -- the wait for the loop's own page extractions, the
    one round of owed re-asks and how many calls it asked, and the slowest page
    -- so the post-loop tail can be sized from a run's own events."""
    completer = ScriptedCompleter(
        decisions=_owed_decisions(),
        outputs=[
            SubTopicFindingsDraft(findings=[]),
            SubTopicFindingsDraft(findings=[]),
            SubTopicFindingsDraft(findings=[]),
        ],
    )
    outcome = await _run_owed_topic(
        tracker,
        completer,
        body=_owed_bulk_body(),
        selected=MAX_OWED_PASSAGES_PER_BATCH + 2,
    )

    completed = next(
        event
        for event in outcome.state_update["events"]
        if event.event_type == "researcher.sub_topic.completed"
    )
    metadata = completed.metadata
    # One page extraction, then one owed round of MAX_OWED_BATCHES packets.
    assert metadata["owed_calls"] == MAX_OWED_BATCHES
    for key in ("extraction_wait_s", "owed_round_s", "slowest_page_s"):
        assert isinstance(metadata[key], float)
        assert metadata[key] >= 0.0
        assert metadata[key] == round(metadata[key], 1)
````

````python
@dataclass(slots=True)
class ExtractionTimings:
    """Where one sub-topic's tail after its loop went (latency audit O8).

    ``extract_findings`` fills it when it is handed one: the seconds spent
    waiting for the page extractions the loop started (``extraction_wait_s``),
    the one round of owed, cross-topic and dissent re-asks after them
    (``owed_round_s``) and how many calls that round asked (``owed_calls``),
    and the slowest single page extraction (``slowest_page_s``). A field a
    pass never reached stays at zero.
    """

    extraction_wait_s: float = 0.0
    owed_round_s: float = 0.0
    owed_calls: int = 0
    slowest_page_s: float = 0.0


````

### Task 4: O8, part 3: one record per provider call, with its label and tokens

Audit O8: per-stage telemetry has totals, not calls, so no artifact says which call was slow or how many of its tokens were reasoning (audit §9 items 1 and 6). The run's collector now keeps one `CallRecordTelemetry` per call: its label, its start offset from the run's start, its seconds, and its output and reasoning tokens. These go in `StageTelemetry.call_records`, which reaches the quality record's `telemetry` block. A context manager, `call_label`, names the calls of the planner, the researcher's extractions and the two verifier checks (Ambiguity A4); any other call keeps its operation name. The planner, researcher and evidence verifier pins move: the call sites are module code. No request text changes.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/deep_research/agents/evidence_verifier.py` (lines 78-81, 1061-1067, 1441-1448)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/agents/planner.py` (lines 51-55, 3306-3323, 3395-3410)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/agents/researcher.py` (lines 73-77, 3445-3465, 3542-3545, 3558-3561, 3604-3612, 3926-3947, 4209-4212, 4228-4234)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/observability/__init__.py` (lines 21-24, 43-46)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/observability/run_telemetry.py` (lines 28-34, 36-39, 46-50, 97-100, 124-127, 136-139, 264-270, 277-280, 312-315)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/utils/types.py` (lines 2487-2490, 2495-2499, 2508-2511)
- Test: `tests/agent_fakes.py` (lines 16-20, 194-197)
- Test: `tests/test_agents/test_evidence_verifier.py` (lines 63-67, 2471-2472)

- Test: `tests/test_evaluation/test_config.py` (lines 1000-1004, 1165-1169, 1262-1266)
- Test: `tests/test_observability_run_telemetry.py` (lines 7-10, 558-559)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `RunTelemetryCollector.record_call(*, agent, operation, seconds, output_tokens, ...)` (`src/deep_research/observability/run_telemetry.py`), `StageTelemetry` (`src/deep_research/utils/types.py`).
- Produces: `class CallRecordTelemetry(ContractModel)` with `label: str`, `start_offset_s: float`, `seconds: float`, `output_tokens: int = 0`, `reasoning_tokens: int = 0`; `StageTelemetry.call_records: tuple[CallRecordTelemetry, ...] = ()`; `call_label(label: str) -> ContextManager[None]` and `current_call_label() -> str | None`, exported from `deep_research.observability`; `tests.agent_fakes.LabelRecordingCompleter` (records `current_call_label()` at each call). Task 18's stage replay reads these records from its own collector.

````python
class LabelRecordingCompleter(ScriptedCompleter):
    """A ``ScriptedCompleter`` that also records, for each structured request,
    the call label its caller bound around it (latency audit O8)."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        decisions: Sequence[ReActDecision | BaseException] = (),
        outputs: Sequence[BaseModel | BaseException] = (),
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(decisions=decisions, outputs=outputs)
        self.labels: list[str | None] = []

    async def complete_structured(
        self,
        messages: Sequence[ChatMessage],
        schema: type[Any],
        *,
        agent_name: str | None = None,
        max_tokens: int | None = None,
        reasoning_effort: str | None = None,
    ) -> Any:
        self.labels.append(current_call_label())
        return await super().complete_structured(
            messages,
            schema,
            agent_name=agent_name,
            max_tokens=max_tokens,
            reasoning_effort=reasoning_effort,
        )


````

````python
@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_the_two_checks_name_their_calls_for_the_call_records(
    tracker: Tracker,
) -> None:
    """Latency audit O8: a Context Check and a Statement Check call carry their
    own names in the run's call records, so a slow verifier call says which
    check it was."""
    read = make_read()
    finding = make_finding(read, SNIPPET, figures=[figure("10.4", "GW", "2024", "actual")])
    context = LabelRecordingCompleter(outputs=[_confirm_reply])
    async with tracker.session_span("session-1", "question"):
        await _evidence_verifier(tracker, context).run(
            _state(raw_findings=[finding], read_records={read.read_id: read})
        )

    statements = LabelRecordingCompleter(outputs=[_confirm_statement_reply])
    await check_statements(
        statements,
        [_statement_item("S01", "Wood Mackenzie states 18.9 GW.", _statement_finding("18.9"))],
        question="How much storage?",
    )

    assert context.labels == ["context_check"]
    assert statements.labels == ["statement_check"]
````

````python
@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_every_plan_side_call_is_named_for_the_call_records(
    tracker: Tracker,
) -> None:
    """Latency audit O8: each plan-side request is named in the run's call
    records -- the draft, the lint repair, the review, the review repair and
    the confirming review -- so a slow planner call can be attributed."""
    lint_repair = LabelRecordingCompleter(
        decisions=[finish("No lookup needed.", "Three angles matter.")],
        outputs=[_stale_draft(), _sorting_plan(), _review()],
    )
    async with tracker.session_span("session-1", "q"):
        await _planner(tracker, lint_repair).run(
            _state("What are the current interconnection constraints?")
        )

    review_repair = LabelRecordingCompleter(
        decisions=[finish("No lookup needed.", "Three angles matter.")],
        outputs=[
            _sorting_plan(),
            _review(
                sound=False,
                missing_dimensions=["siting and permitting"],
                repair_instruction="Add a sub-topic for siting and permitting.",
            ),
            _sorting_plan(),
            _review(),
        ],
    )
    async with tracker.session_span("session-1", "q"):
        await _planner(tracker, review_repair).run(
            _state("What limits grid-scale battery storage deployment?")
        )

    assert lint_repair.labels == ["plan_draft", "plan_repair", "plan_review"]
    assert review_repair.labels == [
        "plan_draft",
        "plan_review",
        "plan_review_repair",
        "plan_confirming_review",
    ]
````

````python
def test_each_call_keeps_its_own_record_with_its_label() -> None:
    """Latency audit O8: a stage keeps one record per call, in the order the
    calls returned -- the operation name its caller bound, or the call's own
    operation when none was bound -- with its start and its tokens."""
    collector = RunTelemetryCollector()
    with call_label("plan_draft"):
        collector.record_call(
            agent="planner",
            operation="structured_output",
            seconds=0.0,
            output_tokens=120,
            configured_cap=393_216,
            truncated=False,
            reasoning_tokens=80,
        )
    collector.record_call(
        agent="planner",
        operation="react_tool_turn",
        seconds=0.0,
        output_tokens=10,
        configured_cap=393_216,
        truncated=False,
    )

    [stage] = collector.snapshot().stages
    assert [
        (record.label, record.output_tokens, record.reasoning_tokens)
        for record in stage.call_records
    ] == [("plan_draft", 120, 80), ("react_tool_turn", 10, 0)]
    first, second = stage.call_records
    assert 0.0 <= first.start_offset_s <= second.start_offset_s
    assert current_call_label() is None
````

````python


# The operation name a caller bound around its provider call (latency audit
# O8): ``record_call`` reads it, so a stage's per-call records say which of an
# agent's calls was slow without any provider signature changing.
_CALL_LABEL: ContextVar[str | None] = ContextVar(
    "deep_research_call_label", default=None
)


@contextmanager
def call_label(label: str) -> Iterator[None]:
    """Name the provider calls made inside this block, for the call records."""
    token = _CALL_LABEL.set(label)
    try:
        yield
    finally:
        _CALL_LABEL.reset(token)


def current_call_label() -> str | None:
    """The operation name bound around the call being recorded, if any."""
    return _CALL_LABEL.get()


def cap_key_for(agent: str | None, operation: LLMOperation) -> str:
    """The config key that bounds one provider call's output tokens."""
````

````python
class CallRecordTelemetry(ContractModel):
    """One provider call that returned, as the latency audit (O8) reads it.

    ``label`` is what the call was for: the operation name its caller bound
    (``plan_draft``, ``page_extraction``, ``context_check`` ...) or, when the
    caller bound none, the call's operation (``structured_output``,
    ``react_tool_turn``, ``chat``). ``start_offset_s`` is when the call started,
    in seconds after the run's collector was created; ``seconds`` is its wall
    time, retries included.
    """

    model_config = ConfigDict(
        extra="forbid", str_strip_whitespace=True, validate_default=True, frozen=True
    )

    label: str = Field(min_length=1)
    start_offset_s: float = Field(ge=0.0)
    seconds: float = Field(ge=0.0)
    output_tokens: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)
    reasoning_tokens: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)


class StageTelemetry(ContractModel):
````

### Task 5: O1: `agents.verifier_concurrency` 16 → 64

Audit O1 (§3.1): Tamil's 34 Context Check batches queued behind 16 slots, so the slowest call (217.5 s) started about 72 s in. With every batch in flight at once, the verifier takes about as long as its slowest call. The batches, their order and their prompts are unchanged: it is a config value (`config.yaml:123`), and the guard proves no request moves. The writer's Statement Check shares the setting; with 11 batches it is unaffected. The one test that asserts the value loads the strict config, so its commands set placeholder secrets (Conventions).

**Files:**
- Modify: `config.yaml` (lines 121-125)
- Test: `tests/test_config.py` (lines 1403-1407)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `AgentRuntimeConfig.verifier_concurrency` (`src/deep_research/utils/config.py:323`; no upper bound).
- Produces: `config.yaml` `agents.verifier_concurrency: 64`.

### Task 6: O2: finalize writes its memory in one batch, with one ONNX session

Audit O2 (§3.2): `_publish` awaits `publish_finding` once per cited finding (`src/deep_research/graph/nodes.py:554`, `:633`). Each one goes through its own `save_many([entry])` (`src/deep_research/memory/long_term.py:220-224`), and the local embedder rebuilds chromadb's default ONNX function per provider instance (`src/deep_research/providers/embeddings.py:148-160`). Tamil's finalize spent 42 s on this.

**The batch path.**
- The memory bridge, the `save_to_memory` tool and the report writer each gain a batch method.
- `_publish` builds every cited finding's payload exactly as before and writes them with one `save_many`.
- When the batch fails, or the publisher has no batch method, it falls back to today's loop (P4).

**The ONNX session.** chromadb's `DefaultEmbeddingFunction` builds its ONNX model again on every call. The embedder now keeps one `ONNXMiniLM_L6_V2` per provider instead: the very model `DefaultEmbeddingFunction` builds (a test proves it). It is built on first use and called under a lock, because callers reach it from worker threads.

**What stays the same.** The entries and vectors are unchanged: memory only feeds later runs, so no request of this run moves. The report writer's pin moves.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/deep_research/agents/report_writer.py` (lines 76-80, 3334-3337)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/graph/__init__.py` (lines 39-42, 136-139)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/graph/nodes.py` (lines 429-432, 576-580, 627-634)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/providers/embeddings.py` (lines 11-14, 116-123, 135-140, 149-154, 157-160)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/runtime/memory_bridge.py` (lines 15-19, 73-76, 87-91, 93-103)
- Modify: `src/deep_research/tools/memory_tools.py` (lines 15-18, 22-25, 45-49, 58-61, 67-70)
- Test: `tests/research_fakes.py` (lines 83-86, 90-93)
- Test: `tests/test_evaluation/test_config.py` (lines 1379-1383)
- Test: `tests/test_graph/test_nodes.py` (lines 57-60, 1532-1535, 1739-1740)
- Test: `tests/test_providers_embeddings.py` (end)
- Test: `tests/test_runtime/test_memory_bridge.py` (lines 3-7, 172-173)
- Test: `tests/test_tools/test_memory_tools.py` (end)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `LongTermMemory.save_many(entries) -> int`, `ReportPublisher` (`src/deep_research/graph/nodes.py`), `SaveToMemoryTool.execute(...)`.
- Produces: `LongTermMemoryBridge.save_many(findings) -> list[str]` (raises `RuntimeError("...findings were not stored")` when the store writes fewer); `SaveToMemoryTool.save_many(findings: Sequence[Mapping[str, JsonValue]]) -> ToolResult`; `ReportWriterAgent.publish_findings(*, findings) -> ToolResult` (a failed result of type `batch_unsupported` when its tool cannot batch); `@runtime_checkable class BatchReportPublisher(ReportPublisher, Protocol)` with `publish_findings`, exported from `deep_research.graph`; `LocalEmbeddingProvider` holding one ONNX model per provider; `tests.research_fakes.FakeMemory.save_many` and `.batches`.

````python
class _BatchPublisher(FakePublisher):
    """A publisher that can save every cited finding in one write."""

    def __init__(self, *, fail_batch: bool = False) -> None:
        super().__init__()
        self._fail_batch = fail_batch
        self.batches: list[list[str]] = []

    async def publish_findings(self, *, findings) -> ToolResult:
        self.batches.append([content for content, _ in findings])
        if self._fail_batch:
            return ToolResult(
                tool_name="save_to_memory",
                success=False,
                error=ToolError(type="RuntimeError", message="The batch was not saved."),
                latency_ms=0.0,
            )
        return ToolResult(
            tool_name="save_to_memory",
            success=True,
            data={"entry_ids": [f"entry-{i}" for i, _ in enumerate(findings)]},
            latency_ms=0.0,
        )


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_an_accepted_report_saves_its_cited_findings_in_one_batch() -> None:
    """Latency audit O2: a publisher that can batch gets one write for every
    cited finding, and the published event still counts each one."""
    publisher = _BatchPublisher()

    result = await finalize_report_node(publisher)(dump_state(_finalized_state()))

    assert publisher.batches == [[SNIPPET]]
    assert publisher.memory_writes == 0  # no one-by-one write was needed
    assert load_state(result).events[-2].metadata["memory_writes"] == 1


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_a_failed_batch_falls_back_to_one_write_per_finding() -> None:
    """When the batch fails, each finding is written on its own, exactly as
    before O2, so each failure is still recorded against its own finding."""
    publisher = _BatchPublisher(fail_batch=True)

    result = await finalize_report_node(publisher)(dump_state(_finalized_state()))
    state = load_state(result)

    assert publisher.batches == [[SNIPPET]]
    assert publisher.saved_findings == [SNIPPET]
    assert state.events[-2].metadata["memory_writes"] == 1
    assert state.errors == []
````

````python
class _CountingModel:
    """Stands in for chromadb's ONNX model; counts how often it is built."""

    built = 0

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        type(self).built += 1

    def __call__(self, input):  # noqa: A002 - chromadb's parameter name
        return [[0.5, 0.25] for _ in input]


def test_the_local_model_is_built_once_and_reused(monkeypatch) -> None:
    """Latency audit O2: one ONNX model per provider, however many calls."""
    import chromadb.utils.embedding_functions.onnx_mini_lm_l6_v2 as onnx_module

    from deep_research.providers import LocalEmbeddingProvider

    _CountingModel.built = 0
    monkeypatch.setattr(onnx_module, "ONNXMiniLM_L6_V2", _CountingModel)
    provider = LocalEmbeddingProvider()

    provider.embed_documents(["alpha", "beta"])
    provider.embed_query("gamma")
    provider.embed_documents(["delta"])

    assert _CountingModel.built == 1


def test_the_kept_model_is_the_one_chromadbs_default_function_builds(
    monkeypatch,
) -> None:
    """The vectors cannot change: chromadb's ``DefaultEmbeddingFunction`` --
    what this provider used before O2 -- builds this same model class on every
    call, so keeping one instance changes only how often it is built."""
    import chromadb.utils.embedding_functions as chroma_embedding_functions
    import chromadb.utils.embedding_functions.onnx_mini_lm_l6_v2 as onnx_module

    _CountingModel.built = 0
    monkeypatch.setattr(onnx_module, "ONNXMiniLM_L6_V2", _CountingModel)
    default = chroma_embedding_functions.DefaultEmbeddingFunction()

    default(["alpha"])
    default(["beta"])

    assert _CountingModel.built == 2
````

````python
class _CountingCollection(FakeCollection):
    """A ``FakeCollection`` that counts its upserts."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        super().__init__()
        self.upserts = 0

    def upsert(
        self,
        *,
        ids: Sequence[str],
        documents: Sequence[str],
        embeddings: Sequence[Sequence[float]],
        metadatas: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]],
    ) -> None:
        self.upserts += 1
        super().upsert(
            ids=ids, documents=documents, embeddings=embeddings, metadatas=metadatas
        )


_BATCH = [
    (
        f"Finding {index} was reached in 2025.",
        {
            "source_url": f"https://example.org/{index}",
            "source_title": "Example",
            "timestamp": "2026-09-30T00:00:00+00:00",
            "verification": "verified",

@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_a_batch_stores_exactly_what_one_save_per_finding_stores(
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
) -> None:
    """Latency audit O2: one ``save_many`` of three findings stores the same
    ids, documents, metadata and vectors as three single saves, and reaches
    the store in one upsert instead of three."""

    async def stored(batch: bool) -> _CountingCollection:
        ids = iter(f"entry-{index}" for index in range(3))
        monkeypatch.setattr(
            memory_bridge_module, "uuid4", lambda: SimpleNamespace(hex=next(ids))
        )
        collection = _CountingCollection()
        bridge = LongTermMemoryBridge(
            LongTermMemory(collection=collection, embeddings=FakeEmbeddings()),
            session_id="session-1",
        )
        if batch:
            assert await bridge.save_many(_BATCH) == ["entry-0", "entry-1", "entry-2"]
        else:
            for content, metadata in _BATCH:
                await bridge.save(content, metadata)
        return collection

    one_by_one = await stored(batch=False)
    batched = await stored(batch=True)

    assert batched.records == one_by_one.records
    assert (one_by_one.upserts, batched.upserts) == (3, 1)


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_a_batch_the_store_refuses_raises() -> None:
    collection = FakeCollection()
    collection.fail_on.add("upsert")
    bridge = LongTermMemoryBridge(
        LongTermMemory(collection=collection, embeddings=FakeEmbeddings()),
        session_id="session-1",
    )

    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError, match="findings were not stored"):
        await bridge.save_many(_BATCH)
````

````python
class _BatchMemory(FakeMemory):
    """The tool's backend, with the batch write the bridge provides."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        super().__init__()
        self.batches: list[list[tuple[str, Mapping[str, object]]]] = []

    async def save_many(
        self, findings: Sequence[tuple[str, Mapping[str, object]]]
    ) -> list[str]:
        self.batches.append(list(findings))
        return [f"entry-{index}" for index, _ in enumerate(findings)]


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_save_many_writes_a_batch_in_one_backend_call_and_one_span() -> None:
    """Latency audit O2: several findings, one backend write, one tool span."""
    memory = _BatchMemory()
    tracker = RecordingTracker()
    tool = SaveToMemoryTool(tracker, memory)  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    result = await tool.save_many(
        [("First finding", {"confidence": 0.9}), ("Second finding", {})]
    )

    assert result.success is True
    assert result.data == {"entry_ids": ["entry-0", "entry-1"]}
    assert memory.batches == [
        [("First finding", {"confidence": 0.9}), ("Second finding", {})]
    ]
    assert memory.save_calls == []
    assert tracker.calls == [
        {"name": "save_to_memory", "inputs": {"finding_count": 2}}
    ]


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_save_many_refuses_the_whole_batch_for_one_invalid_finding() -> None:
    memory = _BatchMemory()
    tool = SaveToMemoryTool(RecordingTracker(), memory)  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    result = await tool.save_many([("A finding", {}), ("   ", {})])

    assert result.success is False
    assert result.error is not None
    assert result.error.type == "ValidationError"
    assert memory.batches == []


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_save_many_fails_as_a_result_on_a_backend_without_a_batch_write() -> None:
    """A backend with only ``save`` makes the batch a failed result, never an
    exception, so the finalizer's one-by-one fallback can take over."""
    tool = SaveToMemoryTool(RecordingTracker(), FakeMemory())  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    result = await tool.save_many([("A finding", {})])

    assert result.success is False
    assert result.error is not None
    assert result.error.type == "AttributeError"
````

````python
    async def save_many(
        self,
        findings: Sequence[tuple[str, Mapping[str, JsonValue]]],

        """Store several findings in one write; return their entry ids in order.

        Latency audit O2: the entries are exactly the ones ``save`` builds, one
        by one, and the store embeds them in one batch and upserts them in one
        call. Any entry the store refuses fails the whole call, so a caller
        that must record each finding's own failure writes them one by one
        instead.
        """
        entries = [self._entry(content, metadata) for content, metadata in findings]
        if await self._memory.save_many(entries) != len(entries):
            raise RuntimeError(
                "long-term memory is unavailable, so the findings were not "
                "stored"
            )
        return [entry.entry_id for entry in entries]
````
